### Page 1

EQUITY: TECHNOLOGY
Panjit International   2481.TW 2481 TT
Global Markets Research
11 September 2026
Favorable pricing, product mix to elevate 2H26-2027F profit margins
New AI datacenter orders remain major growth driver
More positive on Panjit’s AI datacenter power MOSFET supply chain position 
Our recent market survey suggests sustained tight supply of select power discrete 
products in 2H26F, such as power/small signal MOSFET, and diodes. Future Electronics’ 
monthly report indicates lengthened lead times for many MOSFET and diode variants in 
Jul/Aug 2026, along with an uptrend in pricing (Fig 5). On the other hand, SDI’s (2351 TT, 
Buy) management indicated that IDM customers are placing more lead frame orders to the 
company with favorable pricing in 2H26E, in order to meet stronger AI-datacenter demand and 
to secure market share. As some products’ lead times have reached historical record levels 
(for e.g., select Schottky/switch diodes and LV/HV MOSFET lead times reached 52 weeks in 
Aug 2026, vs 20 weeks a year ago), and with limited capacity expansion among IDMs/ 
foundries in the near term, we expect the tight supply to continue in 2H26-1H27F. We also 
expect such a benign environment to be a tailwind for vendors to pass-through cost increases. 
We expect Panit’s 2H26-2027F revenue growth and margin expansion to sustain
Panjit’s 2Q26 revenue grew 16% q-q, better than management’s target, and was driven 
by improved demand across end markets. 2Q26 GM reached a 19-quarter high at 33.2% 
on a greater revenue scale, better product mix, and favorable pricing. We expect the 
revenue and profit margin q-q momentum to sustain in 2H26-2027F, as the company has 
secured more orders from the AI datacenter power discrete market, along with price hikes 
(mainly to pass-through rising fab manufacturing and material costs). We expect 3Q26-
4Q26F revenue to grow 10%/5% q-q, with AI datacenter related revenue contributing 12-14% 
total sales (vs 11% in 1H26). We also assume 2H26-2027F GM to reach 36.5%/37.3% (vs 
33.0% in 1H26) with higher AI and automotive sales exposure, better utilization rates across 
manufacturing sites, along with a third round of price hikes in 4Q26F. 
2026F/27F EPS raised by 3%/10%; TP increased to TWD214, implying 38.5% upside
We raise 2026F/27F EPS, as we assume contribution from more new AI datacenter 
orders mainly starting in 2H26-2027F, and expect a more favorable mix and pricing to 
form better margin profiles. Our TP of TWD214 is based on 25x 2027F EPS of TWD8.55 
(previous TP of TWD195 was based on 25x 2027F EPS of TWD7.79). The stock currently 
trades at 18x/16x 2027F/28F EPS.

### Page 3

Company profile
Panjit is a power semiconductor IDM headquartered in Taiwan. It currently manufactures and sells power discrete (including diodes, MOSFET, and
SIC), Power IC, and solar products. The company’s products are widely used in many end applications including industrial, green energy, consumer
electronics, automotive, computers, power supply, and communication.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD214 is based on 25x 2027F EPS of TWD8.55. We use 25x to derive our TP, vs historical P/E range of 18x since 2018 (The stock

traded in a range from 15-33x when the company was in earnings growth upcycle). The benchmark to the stock is TAIEX.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price
Downside risks: 1) global macroeconomic uncertainty that may drag demand for the company's products, especially auto and industrial; 2) slower-
than-expected supply chain inventory destocking; and 3) pricing competition from peers if global demand is worse than expected.
ESG

Panjit requires itself to uphold the business principles of “innovation, growth, responsibility, and sustainability” in the global semiconductor supply
chain in the face of unprecedented changes in the aspects of global environment and climate, economy and society. The company actively
promotes and continues to make improvement in environmental protection, employee safety and health, labor conditions, moral values, and the
maintenance of energy, striving to become a member of the green enterprises in the world while constantly strengthening ESG (the beauty of the

Environment, the goodness in society, and the truthfulness in governance) actions to respond to changes and challenges in the future with a high
level of dynamics.
Nomura | Panjit International 11 September 2026

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

We attended CIOE and see growing photonics opportunities for

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
USI (601231.SS): ELSFP reinforces our confidence in USI’s optical roadmap.
EugenLight, USI’s photonics subsidiary, showcase its external laser source modules
(ELSFP) during CIOE. We believe EugenLight’s solution stands out on power

efficiency, 8 channel ELSFP around 12W, which should become increasingly
important as optical I/O scales and system-level power constraints tighten. More
importantly, we see ELSFP as a strategically important building block for the
migration toward NPO and ultimately CPO, where moving the laser source outside
the compute/package environment can improve overall system reliability. While
USI’s optical-module business has so far ramped below our earlier expectations

(link), our CIOE discussions suggest that the company has not fallen behind in
ELSFP , giving it another route to participate as architectures evolve beyond
conventional pluggables. Customer design in and production ramp are key things to
watch going forward.
ASMPT (0522.HK): Fluxless TCB addresses two key challenges in optical engine
packaging. Our key takeaway from CIOE is that optical engine  packaging faces two
critical manufacturing challenges – warpage and optical-port contamination –  both

of which could become more pronounced as OEs scale in size and complexity.
Compared with conventional mass reflow, thermo-compression bonding (TCB)
offers better control over the bonding process and can help mitigate warpage,
potentially improving assembly yield and reliability. Meanwhile, ASMPT’s fluxless
TCB solution, enabled by AOR (Atmospheric Oxide Removal), eliminates the need
for flux during bonding, thereby avoiding flux residue and reducing the risk of

contamination at sensitive optical ports. We believe these process advantages make
TCB particularly well suited for next-generation optical engine packaging and
strengthen ASMPT’s positioning as NPO/CPO architectures scale. (Continued)
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley

and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
ELSFP reinforces our confidence in USI’s optical roadmap, which provides another
route to participate in optical architectures
ASMPT's Fluxless TCB could address two key challenges in OE packaging, namely
warpage and optical-port contamination
We see progress in domestic laser chips' capacity expansion and move to higher

power CW; NPO bandwidth improves; VCSEL remains active
Tencent 512-card supernode using 3.2T NPO to be deployed 1Q27; Alibaba targets
4Q26 MP of 3.2T NPO followed by mid-2027 MP of 6.4T UPO
September 13, 2026 09:41 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
 Update
2

Takeaways from other booths: Laser chip capacity expansion and higher power CW 
products were key themes, with Source Photonics outlining significant EML and CW 
capacity expansion for 2027, with its 200mW CW products targeted to support NPO in 
1Q27. Source Photonics also demonstrated an ELSFP for 6.4T application using its in-
house  CW lasers, with 8 outputs delivering ~112mW per channel. Yunling displayed 
200mW and 300mW chips, HiSilicon showcased a 400mW device, and Everbright 
launched a 400mW-at-50°C CW DFB chip. We believe customer qualification and 
sustained volume delivery remain key differentiators as product availability broadens. 
NPO optical-engine bandwidth is progressing from 3.2T to 6.4T, with exhibits from Source 
Photonics and Accelink, and to 7.2T in HiSilicon's integrated-light-source design. VCSEL 
also remains an active NPO route for short-reach applications with HiSilicon displaying a 
3.2T VCSEL NPO. 
Conference takeaways: Tencent outlined a roadmap targeting the deployment of a 512-
acceleratotor supernode using 3.2T NPO in 1Q27, followed by a feasibility assessment in 
2Q27. Alibaba's roadmap targeted 4Q26 mass production of 3.2T NPO modules and 
QFabric switches followed by 6.4T UPO production around mid-2027. Its 6.4T UPO, 
developed with Accelink, is set to be demonstrated at the Sep 2026 Apsara conference.  
Tencent and Alibaba highlighted scale-up as a key near-term opportunity for NPO, citing its 
balance of bandwidth density, power consumption, supplier openness and delivery 
readiness. Industry participants also argued that NPO could remain a viable long-term 
architecture rather than a transitional one. For scale out, pluggables may remain the 
favored solution for broad deployment by some industry participants. Huawei's roadmap 
has progressed from 50G VCSEL solution in its 384-accelerator A3 supernode to 100G 
VCSEL LPO solution for its A5 supernode up to 8,192 accelerators. HiSilicon showcased 
both 3.2T VCSEL NPO and 7.2T SiPh NPO with integrated CW sources as potential future 
architectures.
Morgan Stanley Asia Limited (“Morgan Stanley”) is currently acting as exclusive financial 
advisor to ASMPT Limited ("ASMPT") in relation to its assessment of strategic options for 
its SMT Solutions Segment (the “Assessment”), as announced on January 21, 2026. There is 
no assurance that the Assessment will result in a transaction, nor as to the timing or terms 
thereof. ASMPT has agreed to pay fees to Morgan Stanley for its financial services. Please 
refer to the notes at the end of the report.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

## Document 082: 260914_gs_umt

Source PDF: all report pdf/260914_gs_umt.pdf

### Page 1

UMT provides rectangular waveguides, which are conducting pipes with a 
rectangular cross-section used to guide the propagation of microwave or mmwave 
signals. Different shapes and combinations of waveguides can form various RF 
passive components, such as filter / diplexer, coupler, isolator, antenna, power 
amplifiers (PAs), etc. The company serves leading global LEO satellite operators, and 
its waveguides can be seen in LEO satellites / payloads (main), and gateways in 
ground stations.

### Page 2

LEO satellite launches update: Starlink launched 261 LEO satellites in Aug 2026, or 
+13% MoM. The number of satellites per launch continues to increase from 24 satellites 
per launch in May 2026 to 26 satellites per launch in Aug 2026, and at 27 satellites per 
launch so far in Sep 2026, reaffirming our positive view on the constellation construction 
cycle. On Sep 18, SpaceX’s Starship V3 is scheduled to have the 3rd flight and the first 
Starship flight to deploy satellites (a group of Starlink satellites) into stable LEO, and 
should have the 4th flight of Starship V3 within 2026 (link). Starship V3 is a reusable 
vehicle, with LEO capacity at 100k kg, or 339% higher than Falcon 9 Block 5 (22.8kg), the 
current vehicle that Starlink is using. The vehicle migration would not only accelerate 
Starlink’s satellite launches but also release the legacy vehicle (e.g. Falcon 9 Block 5) to 
other LEO satellite operators, facilitating the overall industry’s satellite launches. For 
example, Amazon has planned 10 launches (LF-04 to LF-13) in 2029, using Falcon 9 
Block 5. Assuming 27 satellites per launch via the vehicle, the 10 missions would bring 
270 satellites (vs. 180 / 216 satellites in 2025 / 7M26 that Amazon added). Amazon is 
also increasing its satellites per launch: the upcoming pipeline shows 35-48 satellites per 
launch vs. 29-36 satellites per launch in May to July 2026, or increasing by 28% on avg. 
The acceleration of LEO satellite launches reaffirms our positive view on UMT.

Earnings revision: We factor in Aug revenues, and raise net income by 1% in 2026-28E. 
The revenue raise is to reflect our positive view on accelerating LEO satellite launches 
along with specification upgrade driving up the dollar content. Our GM is reduced to 
reflect our expectation that the larger scale would lead to lower GM with customers’ 
rising procurement power. Nevertheless, with the company’s product mix upgrade 
toward LEO satellite, the blended GM should continue to increase through 2028E.

Compared to Bloomberg consensus, our net income is in line in 2026E and 13% higher in 
2027E, reflecting our positive view on the LEO satellite constellation construction cycle, 
UMT’s dollar content increase on specification upgrade, and UMT’s leading market 
position considering its strong design capability, commitment in capacity expansion and 
timely services to customers. In Aug, the company announced investment of US$12m 
(NT$388m) in Vietnam and procured US$9.3m of equipment for its Vietnam factory, 
reflecting its continuous capacity expansion and diversification. The company also 
announced it would set up a subsidiary in the US with investment of up to US$10m 
(NT$323m), and we expect the subsidiary could support the company to serve its major 
customers in a timely manner.

### Page 4

Valuation: We continue to derive our target price based on a discounted P/E, and based 
on 2029E (methodology unchanged). We continue to derive the target P/E multiple from 
the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the satellite supply chain, and we remove outliers 
(those below 0.3x, which are mostly telecom operators who face competition from LEO 
satellite operators or having consumer electronics exposure which suffered from high 
memory costs). Our target 2029E discounted P/E multiple is at 37.0x (unchanged) which 
is based on peers’ avg. PEG&M, and UMT’s 2029-30E avg. NI YoY and OPM. We apply the 
37.0x multiple to 2029E EPS and discount it back to 2027E via a COE of 7.8% (no 
change). Our target price is at NT$2,557 (vs. NT$2,513 previously), implying 61x 2027E 
P/E, which is above the company’s average +1 stv. P/E of 42x, reflecting our positive view 
on UMT’s product mix upgrade toward LEO satellites. Maintain Buy.

### Page 5

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 37.0x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,557. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

## Document 088: 260914_ubs_TW-summit-day-I-hardware

### Page 1

ab 14 September 2026
Global Research
First Read
APAC Tech
TW Summit Day 1 on Hardware/Components
Chassis/rail kit suppliers have started to ramp for new server platform
One chassis supplier has noted that, post solid inventory built in Q2, ODMs have slowed
down pull-in momentum for one of the major US CSP ASIC suppliers (likely AWS, in our
view) in Q3 due to supply bottleneck for memory and cooling components. Inventories
are anticipated to be digested by September and anticipate ODM pull-in momentum to

resume in September/October. Another supplier noted AWS general server volumes
were also down in Q2 on component mismatch (citing memory, CPU, and MLCC
shortages), but seeing shipment coming back from Q3. Meanwhile, one supplier noted
it has begun to ramp chassis shipments for NVDA Vera Rubin platform with enterprise
started pull-in earlier in Q226, followed by major CSPs' orders in Q326; and another
supplier noted it anticipates to start shipping VR rail kits in volumes  in Q326. Both

suppliers noted they have witnessed stronger than expected strength for NVDA GB as
well as HGX platform in H126; and picking up strong momentum from enterprise
customers as well. The supply chain continues to point to robust AI server growth and
healthy general server demand into H226; consistent with UBS expectations for King
Slide and Chenbro AI server volumes to take off strongly into Sep/Q426, backed by their
leading positions in key GPU and ASIC platforms, with favourable content uplifts.

Other highlights from the day one hardware meetings
1) ZDT anticipates optical module and server business will strengthen from Q226 and
more than doubled in 2026 (optical module/server/IC substrate sales mix  up from 12%
in 2025 to 20%+/45-50% in 2026/2030F). It is building out its high-end PCB capacity,
investing NT$80bn in 2026 with more strategic investments in 2027/2028 to focus on
iHDI/mSAP, HLC, IC substrates, and advanced FPCs, with production to come online in

phases through 2030. 2) Nan Juen noted Eagle Stream remain the mainstream this year
but Birch Stream platform has started to take off in Q2/Q3. Small volumes of Vera Rubin
rail kits started in Q3  with potential ASIC project to kick off in Q4. 3) Chenbro reiterated
+40% YoY 2026 sales target and remained optimistic on 2027 outlook  supported by
strong chassis momentum from both CSPs/enterprises and rack business expansion
(guiding rack sales contribution from 10-15% in 2026 to 15%+ in 2027F). 4)  Lenovo

targets to outgrow its view for 10%-20% YoY industry PC unit decline while data center
business is seeing strong momentum from both CSP and ESMBs with strong AI order
backlog and ISG restructuring move demonstrating strong margin expansion. 5)
Compal sees stable general server demand and AI server picking up streams from
neocloud and enterprise customers while seeing some progress with hyperscalers. It
expects 2026 PC shipments to decline mid teens YoY with consumer models under

pressure into H226 and seeing de-spec on SSD/DRAM for price-sensitive products. 6)
Inventec saw strong growth across AI servers (B300 and higher rack business) and
general servers including two top ASIC CPU projects; and expects NB sales to grow
double digits YoY in 2026F as it volumes outgrow industry (down HSD YoY vs. market
units down teens) on higher commercial exposure (60-70% mix) and higher ASPs.
Stock implications
We stay positive on hardware/component suppliers that commands leading position

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Investing in the technology sector involves a high degree of risk. Rapid technological changes,
increasing competition and exposure to macroeconomic cycles are among the many risks
faced by investors in technology stocks. Moreover, it is extremely difficult to project the
financial results of tech companies since their operating models are highly volatile and
unpredictable. Finally, valuing technology stocks can prove challenging, as neither traditional

nor non-traditional valuation measures have provided much insight into how these stocks
trade. Valuation methodologies in the sector include target price-book-multiples based on
the long term average ROE and the cost of equity.

Options and futures are not suitable for all investors, and trading in these instruments is considered risky and may be appropriate only for sophisticated investors. Prior to

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.
Important Information About Sustainable Investing Strategies: Sustainable investing strategies aim to consider and incorporate environmental, social and governance
(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

ab 14 September 2026
Global Research
First Read
WIN Semiconductors
2026 Taiwan Summit: Optical and Infrastructure
to drive mid-to-long-term growth
Q326 revenue tracking in line with guidance
WIN Semi attended our 2026 Taiwan Summit on Sep 14th. August revenue of NT
$1,937mn (+2% QoQ and +31%YoY) is tracking at 65% of both UBSe/Street at +12%
QoQ. WIN Semi maintained its Q326F revenue guidance of low-teens QoQ  growth,
mainly driven by optical and infrastructure segments. It noted revenue outlook from

WiFi has improved from a previously flattish QoQ  to sequentially QoQ growth as a result
of stronger router demand, and cellular remains flattish. Management did not provide
Q426 guidance, noting that demand will partly depend on smartphone seasonality,
although it remains more constructive on the growth outlook for aerospace and non-
smartphone optical markets. In terms of  capex, WIN Semi budgeted NT$2-3bn of capex
spending in 2026, primarily for GaN and InP expansion.

Laser diode remains in qualification but photodiode ramp accelerates
Management noted that its CW lasers and EML remains under  qualification with more
meaningful revenue contribution from 2027 and beyond. While WIN Semi is engaging
with a broader customer base for laser diode than for photodiode, management noted
its near-term optical growth should be driven primarily by the ongoing photodiode
ramp, with volumes expected to increase sequentially both MoM and QoQ through

H226F and continue strengthening into 2027F. Management also noted that its 50G
and 100G VCSEL solutions are already in production, providing additional support for
non-smartphone optical growth. As a result, smartphone-related optical is expected to
decline from 60-70% of optical revenue historically to around 50% in Q326F as non-
smartphone optical applications expand. Management maintained its target for AI/
datacom revenue (driver, PIC, laser diode, photodiode, and etc) exposure to reach high-

single digits by end-2026, up from a low-single-digit contribution in 2025.
Rising aerospace content strengthens long-term growth outlook
Management remains constructive on the long-term outlook and its margin profile as
the business mix shifts toward higher-margin optical and aerospace applications.
Aerospace revenue has increased to over 30% of infrastructure revenue (Infrastructure
was 30-35% of total sales in Q226F), driven by expanding LEO deployments, direct-to-

cell connectivity, and rising RF content as additional frequency bands and advanced
phased-array antennas are adopted, as well as migration from GaAs to GaN platform.
Management views aerospace and optical datacom as key medium- to long-term
growth drivers, supporting a more diversified revenue mix and improved margin profile.
Valuation: Maintain Buy rating with a PE-based price target of NT$500
We rate WIN Semi with  BUY and  PT of NT$500, based on 40x 2027-28E average PE. We

believe WIN Semi is well positioned to capture rising infrastructure and optical growth.

Company Description
Established in 1999, WIN Semiconductors is a leading foundry for compound semiconductor
wafers globally, with 43k 6-inch wafer capacity per month as of end-2022. While it offers
different types of compound semiconductors (GaAs, GaN, InP, etc.), the GaAs compound
contributes the majority of its revenue. Its products are used to make signal power amplifiers

that customers use in networking equipment and communication devices, including base
stations and smartphones.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value WIN Semi using PE relative to its historical trading range and peer average.
Investing in WIN Semi involves risks that include, but are not limited to, rapid technological
changes, increasing competition, exposure to macroeconomic cycles and uncertain
enddemand. Slower wireless technology migration and poor performances by customers

could also significantly affect WIN Semi’s fundamental performance. WIN Semi’s LEO satellite
customers may face execution challenges in launching satellites, which will impact WIN
Semi's infrastructure revenue growth outlook. On the other hand, its progress on developing
and qualifying the optical communication laser diodes will have significant impact to its
earnings outlook and market sentiment. Moreover, it is extremely difficult to project the

financial results of tech companies, since their operating models are highly volatile and
unpredictable.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

## Document 109: 260918_ubs_wiwynn

ab 18 September 2026
Global Research
First Read
Wiwynn
Reaffirm the strong   growth through 2027
New platforms support AI strength into 2027 while general server slower
before next ramp in 2027
We maintain our Buy on Wiwynn with a price target of $ 2,470  and also in the
UBS regional most preferred tech list. Amid the market noise, we stay positive on the
company's growth, with catalysts from liquid cooled ASIC servers in initial production
and ramping up further through 2027 and Helios racks on track as a new project

commencing from late 2026E through 2027. We do see general server cooling into Sep
and Q426 to offset the ASIC server ramp before picking up from Q127 as customers
transition to the next-generation Clearwater Forest/Venice platforms.
August sales ahead supported by general server growth
Wiwynn August sales of NT$144.3bn, +22.6% MoM, 50.4% YoY tracked ahead at
74%/73% UBSe/Street of +27%/+29% QoQ estimate in Q3. We believe the growth is
driven mainly driven by pre-build ahead of further price hikes. The company is seeing

tight supply across major component includes memory, CPU and PCB, but the impact on
deliveries has been limited through August. Since Q226, the company has booked
general server memory on its balance sheet rather than through sales and COGs, which
lifted gross margin in Q2. We factor in lower NRE and mix shifting from general server
now the highest margin with the consigned memory toward ASIC to lower GM/OpM off
the higher Q2 base of 9.3/7.3% to 8.8/6.8% in Q3 and 8.7/6.7% in Q4.

Multiple drivers support another strong year in 2027
We maintain 2027 revenue growth +89% YoY to NT$2,491bn, reflecting strength
across ASICs further ramping and rising liquid penetration toward 30% this generation,
GPUs with Helios racks ramping to 6-7k and general server continuing double digit
volume growth. We keep more conservative on Vera Rubin following limited shipments
on GB300 although should begin shipping in small volumes in Q426 and contributing

~2% of sales in 2027.
Reiterate our BUY with  price target of NT$2,470 (7,350 pre-split)
Following the recent 3-1 stock split, all per-share data below are on a post-split basis. We
reiterate our Buy rating and keep our basic EPS of NT$226.4/NT$174.1 reflecting better
margins, intact ASIC and general server ramps and customer AMD project from Q4 into
2027. Our PT of NT$2,470 is based on same 14x P/E on 2027E EPS also with 3% cash
yield (NT$74 cash dividend). We maintain a positive view reflecting Wiwynn's leverage

to ASIC servers, AMD racks, alongside healthy general server demand.

Company Description
Spun off from Wistron in March 2012, Wiwynn designs and manufactures cloud
infrastructure equipment for hyperscaler data centres. Prior to 2015, Wiwynn provided
storage servers and traditional servers. In 2015, Wiwynn began to shift its focus to

manufacturing full rack server for hyperscale data centre clients. Its key clients include US
Facebook (50-60% of total revenue), Microsoft (40-50%) and Amazon. Wiwynn is the
second-largest white-box server manufacturer in terms of shipments. It has production sites
in Mexico, the Czech Republic and China.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's
interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of

shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE multiple methodology.
Key downside risks for Wiwynn include: 1) a slowdown in the US CSP capex cycle; 2) margin
compression due to intense competition; and 3) unfavourable FX movement.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

TSMC: AI growth broadens beyond GPU/ ASIC  — TSMC remains central to nearly
every major AI hardware transition. The most important point is that its AI
opportunity is broader than the GPU/ASIC revenue definition investors typically
focus on. Networking silicon, AI CPUs, silicon photonics and advanced packaging
provide incremental growth vectors, while the underlying compute roadmap
continues migrating rapidly toward leading-edge nodes. We expect stronger 2027

growth for TSMC thanks to full-year contribution from 2nm, increased 3nm AI-
chip volumes and potential pricing increases, creating another powerful
combination of volume, mix and ASP expansion. In terms of competitive
landscape, we think Intel still needs substantial leading-edge capacity for its own
CPUs and appears constrained in its ability to simultaneously support internal
products and large external AI customers. In addition, Intel’s 14A ecosystem and

PDK/customer engagement process would require considerable qualification and
design-cycle time before translating into meaningful external production. For
critical AI products, customers also need predictable multiyear capacity
commitments; TSMC’s manufacturing scale and supply-chain coordination remain
important competitive advantages. Nevertheless, we see potential opportunities
for Intel and UMC’s collaboration at 12nm, that could support products such as

connectivity, display-driver or consumer-related chips, while its advanced
packaging technology could also win specialized programs. However, those
opportunities should be distinguished from a broad challenge to TSMC’s leading-
edge foundry position. Strategically, we think TSMC is becoming more selective
about where it deploys capital and engineering resources. We believe front-end
wafer manufacturing, SoIC and differentiated technologies such as silicon

photonics remain priorities, while more conventional back-end operations are
increasingly suitable for outsourcing. This suggests that continued AI growth can
benefit TSMC directly through leading-edge wafer revenue while simultaneously
creating substantial opportunities for OSAT partners.
TSMC outsourcing creates a structural opportunity for ASEH- Given the tight
supply for both advanced node wafer and CoWoS, we think leading-edge front-
end manufacturing generates the highest strategic value, and we believe TSMC has

an incentive to prioritize wafer fabrication and differentiated packaging
technologies such as SoIC while outsourcing larger portions of more back-end
processing. CoWoS capacity can continue expanding, but TSMC’s own back-end
investment intensity may eventually moderate as qualified OSAT partners assume
more steps. ASE/SPIL are already participating in substrate-related outsourced
work and should gain additional opportunities as the ecosystem matures. Other

Taiwanese packaging companies can also participate, while interposer production
itself could become more distributed across external partners. Importantly, ASEH
is not dependent solely on TSMC outsourcing. It is developing its own full-process
advanced-packaging capabilities, including FoCoS, providing a more direct route
to capture high-value AI packaging revenue. AMD is an important
customer/catalyst for ASEH, in our view, particularly as CPU and heterogeneous-

compute architectures require increasingly sophisticated packaging. ASEH’s
management’s expectation that leading-edge advanced-packaging revenue can
roughly double underscores the potential operating leverage. As chiplets, HBM,
silicon photonics and increasingly complex interconnects proliferate, packaging
content per device rises and the technical barriers to entry increase, potentially
supporting structurally better margins for ASEH.
CPO is emerging as the next major AI infrastructure transition - Co-packaged

optics (CPO) appears to be one of the clearest structural transitions in AI hardware
because it addresses two increasingly severe constraints simultaneously: data
transmission bandwidth and power consumption. We expect Nvidia should be the
initial catalyst, first through Spectrum-X CPO switches and subsequently
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 3

Taiwan Semiconductors
21 September 2026 Citi Research
3
through CPO integration associated with next-generation NVLink scale-up
architectures and Rubin Ultra. Importantly, there are two different CPO adoption
timelines that are often confused. CPO embedded directly alongside GPU/compute
packages could remain two to three years away, but switch-level CPO is
considerably closer and should begin scaling earlier. Moving optical connectivity
closer to the switching silicon can materially reduce electrical I/O distance and

power while supporting higher aggregate bandwidth. During Semicon Taiwan in
early Sep, CPO coupling, optical alignment, fiber-array assembly and testing were
among the most prominent themes. TSMC is developing its COUPE silicon-
photonics/packaging platform, while a broader ecosystem is forming around
optical engines, lasers, fiber-array units (FAUs), receptacles, precision alignment
and test equipment. The most visible near-term bottlenecks appear to be external

laser sources and fiber/FAU capacity, helping explain aggressive expansion by
suppliers such as Lumentum and Corning. The transition could also redistribute
semiconductor supply-chain value as CPO moves more value into advanced
semiconductor packaging, silicon photonics, optical coupling and testing. We
believe Largan’s high precision automation capability will be able to catch CPO
opportunity particularly in the most challenging optical fiber array alignment

process.
Custom ASIC: MediaTek and GUC riding with Google - The Google TPU ecosystem
illustrates both the opportunity and the execution complexity of custom AI silicon.
We believe MediaTek’s role should increase substantially over coming generations,
MediaTek’ s target market share is 15–20% initially but we see more upside ahead.
With current TPU v8 generation, MediaTek’s responsibility is concentrated more
heavily in I/O, SerDes and wafer-handling-related activities; in the subsequent

generation, its scope expands toward portions of the compute implementation as
well. The SerDes configuration itself is also expected to increase, while advanced-
node compute content materially lifts MediaTek’s dollar value per device.
Consequently, revenue can grow much faster than TPU units or market share alone
would suggest. One execution risk is advanced packaging. A future
Google/MediaTek program is expected to utilize Intel’s EMIB-T technology, which is

less proven at very high volume than TSMC’s mature CoWoS ecosystem. Much of
the difficult integration work, however, is distributed across substrate suppliers
and OSAT partners. We think the initial yields and volumes could therefore be
constrained, but the program has strategic importance for Google, Intel and
MediaTek and is likely to proceed despite an early learning curve. And we believe its
existing TPU generations should also continue shipping during the transition,

cushioning the revenue impact. We believe Google’s broader engagement with
AMD and Marvell should not automatically be interpreted as additional TPU
suppliers, in our view, Marvell has important networking and storage relationships
with Google, while AMD opportunities appear more closely associated with CPUs.
More broadly, the ASIC ecosystem remains structurally more fragmented than
Nvidia’s platform. Google is gradually internalizing more silicon expertise, but it still

requires external design-service and implementation partners. We also see
substantial growth of Google’s CPU at GUC, which is fully COT model with back-
end only service.
AI systems and power: Hon Hai benefits from Rubin, while 800VDC adoption
should be gradual- At the system level, Hon Hai remains a major beneficiary of
Nvidia’s rack-scale transition. This year’s growth has been driven largely by higher
AI rack shipments, but next year adds a much higher ASP as the supply chain

transitions from GB300 toward Vera Rubin. Higher rack value means revenue and
profit can rise substantially even if unit growth moderates, provided margins
remain broadly stable. Beyond Nvidia, Hon Hai is also positioning itself to
manufacture custom-ASIC and hyperscaler systems, including CPU- and TPU-
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 4

Taiwan Semiconductors
21 September 2026 Citi Research
4
related racks, which could begin contributing more meaningfully later next year.
Power architecture is another major debate, particularly around 800VDC. The
key distinction is that 800VDC is strategically attractive but not necessarily
mandatory for Rubin or even every Rubin Ultra deployment. Rack power rises
materially from the GB300 generation toward Rubin, requiring changes such as
sidecar power shelves and more sophisticated power distribution. New greenfield

AI data centers and neocloud deployments have stronger incentives to design
around 800VDC because they can optimize the entire facility for future high-
density racks. Existing facilities, however, can continue using alternative
architectures, making adoption gradual rather than immediate. We believe Delta
remains structurally well positioned in high-power data-center infrastructure, but
expectations around the speed of 800VDC adoption may be slower than expected.

The industry also faces a significant engineering learning curve as rack power
density rises, including thermal management and potential overheating issues.
Separately, shortages of power-management ICs and capacitors remain important
constraints regardless of whether a data center uses 800VDC. Analog and power-
semiconductor supply is supported by both AI-driven demand and relatively tight
capacity at IDM suppliers and specialty foundries such as UMC and VIS.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

M
Update
Bizlink  | Asia Pacific
Partnership with ams OSRAM
for Breakthrough Optical
Interconnect Solution
This partnership represents another effort for Bizlink in
diversifying its data interconnect solutions across both the
copper and optical space.
What's new:
• Bizlink announced it will partner with ams OSRAM on next-generation
optical interconnects for the scale-up network.
• The solution will leverage ams OSRAM's 850nm thin-film micro VCSEL

technology (in 25um pitch array) and Bizlink's expertise in fiber-array
integration, optical packaging, and volume production, featuring low latency,
lower power (0.25pJ/bit), wide and slow (32 Gbps), reliable (no failures after
2,000+ hours of testing under stressed conditions), scalable  and
connectorized architecture.
• A demonstration of this solution  will be showcased in the upcoming ECOC
2026 conference in Spain.
Our view:
• We think the industry continues to seek innovative ways of solving the

power consumption issue in data interconnects, including the relocation of
the optical engines (e.g., NPO/CPO) and the alternative light sources (e.g.,
micro LED and micro VCSEL).
• This represent another effort from Bizlink to diversify its data interconnect
solutions across both the copper and optical space, which could help reduce
the risk of technology disruptions.
• We thus see positive implications to Bizlink from this in the mid to long term,

though the revenue contribution might take time.
• Stay constructive on Bizlink for its multiple drivers in the AI deployment
theme, including data/power interconnects and semi cap equipment.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 120: 260922_ubs_GUC

### Page 1

ab 22 September 2026
Global Research
Global Unichip
Better wafer allocation and demand upside
driving H226-2027E earnings growth
Better wafter allocation driving H226-2027E CPU project ramps
We expect CPU projects to remain GUC's main revenue driver in 2026-27E and see
improved wafer allocation into 2027E. We now expect Google CPU volumes to ramp
from 1.3mn units in 2026E to ~4mn units in 2027E, supported by rising TPU shipments
(up 117% YoY in 2027E), a rising CPU-to-TPU ratio, and better wafer allocation from

TSMC. Although GUC's CPU projects carry lower GM, we believe they remain earnings
accretive given the operating leverage from limited incremental OPEX requirements. We
now expect 2026E revenue growth of 94% YoY to NT$66.3 bn (vs prior +90% YoY) and
2027E revenue to grow 146% YoY (with Google CPU contributing 67% of 2027E sales),
up from our prior estimate of 84% YoY. We also expect OPM to expand from 13.2% in
2026E to 13.9% in 2028E. Accordingly, we raise our 2027/28E EPS estimates by

29%/22% and lift our PT to NT$8,660 (from NT$6,700), based on 52x average 2027-
28E EPS. Our 2027/28E EPS estimates are 23%/14% above consensus.
Broadening CSP exposure supports medium-term growth
Beyond the Google CPU ramp, we also see improving demand from Microsoft's Maia
platform, where GUC supplies CPU and the Maia 200 accelerator. Although Maia 300 is
shifting to another supplier, we see extended runway for Maia 200, as well as continued
CPU shipments into 2027E. For the next-gen Maia 400, we believe this remains in the

bidding stage, with GUC maintaining a competitive position over peers. GUC also
secured a 2nm ASIC project from Meta in Q226 for a turnkey revenue ramp from mid-
2028E. These CPU and ASIC projects, along with GUC's strong execution track record,
provide attractive opportunities to engage with top CSPs on accelerator opportunities.
Nevertheless, we do not expect meaningful revenue contribution before 2028.
Automotive to see more significant contribution from mid-2027E

We believe automotive will be another key growth driver for GUC over the medium
term, initially supported by a Chinese EV customer from late-2026, followed by a US EV
maker in H227E. We estimate automotive will contribute 2%/13% of total revenue in
2026E/27E, respectively, increasing further to 18% in 2028E. As GUC offers higher
value-add in the automotive projects vs. Google CPU, we believe they should support
GM and deliver a higher profit contribution relative to sales. In addition, revenue could

see further upside should these solutions expand into robotic applications.
Valuation: Retain BUY, raise PT to NT$8,660
Our NT$8,660 price target is based on 52x average 2027-28E EPS (vs prior 50x), vs
historical 17-84x range, supported by 56% CAGR in earnings over 2025-30E. We
reiterate our Buy rating, as GUC is well positioned for upside in CPU demand, ramp of
automotive projects, other ASICs and emerging optical interconnect opportunities.

### Page 2

Global Unichip UBS ResearchGlobal Unichip (3443.TW)
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Can GUC further grow its AI and high-performance computing sales?
Yes, in our view, supported by multiple drivers. GUC has benefited from being part of TSMC's
ecosystem, with better access to TSMC's technology roadmap, especially on advanced nodes. It has
won several CPU/XPU projects from US hyperscalers and it is also working with leading EV makers on

next-generation autonomous driving ASICs. GUC aims to expand its tier-1 hyperscaler customer base
and we believe there is an opportunity for it to provide advanced packaging services, leveraging the
CoWoS capacity it secured, even though it is not directly involved in the ASIC design.
Q: Are GUC's projects on mature nodes (16nm and above) sustainable?
We think they are likely to remain stable. GUC's customers are sticky on 16nm and higher nodes.
Owing to its strong IP portfolio, which reduces design costs and improves manufacturing yield, these

mature nodes still contribute around 30% of GUC's sales in 2025. While we expect mature nodes to
remain accretive to margins – catering to repeat projects that require little R&D resources – the
growth is likely to be modest at 27% CAGR for 2025-28E vs 104% CAGR for advanced nodes.
Q: Will GUC's OPM recover in 2026-28?
We expect its OPM to show a slight improvement in 2026, as it further ramps the lower-GM Google
CPU project, and incurs a temporary increase in opex due to the new Turing centre. However, we

foresee a more significant improvement in OPM from 2027 as it ramps a higher GM autonomous
driving project from H227, growing  NRE contributions, and restocking of mature projects.
We reiterate our Buy rating on GUC and raise our 2026/27/28E EPS by 0.4%/29%/22%, respectively,
primarily reflecting higher sales contributions from Google's CPU projects. We raise our price target
from NT$6,700 to NT$8,660 based on 52x average 2027-28E EPS (vs a 17-84x historical range),

supported by a 2025-30E EPS CAGR of 56%. Potential catalysts include: 1) further TPU/CPU upside
from the Google project; 2) the continued ramp of Microsoft's AI CPU/ASIC chips and the potential to
win back future-gen projects; 3) upside on new 2nm project wins; 4) a faster ramp of autonomous EV
projects; and 5) advanced NRE opportunities with higher dollar content.
GUC's revenue remained muted in 2023-24, as it shifted its resources from Chinese to US customers

due to BIS export controls. The customer diversification effort began bearing fruit in H125, as GUC
recorded a notable uptick in the turnkey sales to US crypto customers, while the Google CPU ramp
since late Q425 appears sustainable. With the support of its major shareholder TSMC, which has
enabled better access to advanced technology, GUC continues to work with Microsoft, Tesla and
other hyperscalers on custom AI chipsets, which should underpin its  growth in the next few years.

GUC stock rallied 57% in the past three months vs 3% for the TAIEX, on Google TPU news in our
view, and is now trading at 55x 2027E EPS vs its historical 17-84x range. We believe the stock should
continue to re-rate on TPU upside, rising CPU/TPU ratio, multiple automotive project ramps, and
potential upside from wafer allocation. We expect  a 56% CAGR in earnings over 2025-30E and
foresee potential upward revisions in consensus earnings estimates being a catalyst.

GUC is an IC design service company founded in 1998. The company's business is to design chipsets
for its customers who do not have semiconductor capability. It is ~35% owned by TSMC.

Risk to the current share price is skewed (1.7:1) to the upside
GUC is trading at NT$7,615 (as of 21 September 2026).

UPSIDE (NT$12,000.00): Our upside scenario factors in stronger sales growth in 2027-
28E than we forecast in our base case, on the back of better demand for Google's TPU
and US/China automotive projects, more N3 wafer allocation by leading foundries, and
faster penetration of other hyperscalers, lifting 2027/28E sales by 170%/40% YoY. Our
upside OPM assumptions are 13.4%/14.1% for 2027/28E, 0.2ppt above our base case
owing to a limited increase in opex, supporting a 96% CAGR in earnings over 2026-28E.

Our upside scenario valuation of NT$12,000.00 implies a 76x 2027E PE.
BASE (NT$8,660.00): Our base case reflects our expectations of a solid business growth
outlook in 2027-28E, supported by the continued ramp of Google's CPU project, initial
ramp of automotive projects from Q426, and expansion into other hyperscalers. We
forecast 2027/28E sales to grow 146%/34% YoY, and OPM to expand to 13.2%/13.9%
from 12.7% in 2025 on operating leverage, despite its GM potentially normalising to

around 17.8% in 2028 (from 24.8% in 2025) on a higher proportion of sales from the
CPU project, resulting in earnings growth of 83% CAGR over 2026-28E. Our base-case
valuation of NT$8,660.00 implies a 52x 2027-28E average PE.
DOWNSIDE (NT$5,000.00): Our downside scenario factors in slower sales growth in
2027/28E than we forecast in our base case, due to softer growth in Google's CPU due to
a wafer capacity constraint and slower demand for mature process products, dampening

sales growth to 121%/26% YoY. Our downside OPM assumptions are 13.0%/13.7% for
2027/28E, 0.2pp below our base case, resulting in earnings growth decelerating to a
68% CAGR over 2026-28E. Our downside scenario valuation of NT$5,000.00 implies a
41x 2027E P/E.

Company Description
Global Unichip (GUC) is a leading IC design service company in Taiwan, founded in 1998. The
company's business is to design chipsets for customers that do not have IC design capability,
with the goal of reducing entry barriers and risks for chipset design and shortening the time

to market. The company's target to enable more IC design for start-ups and system
companies is complimentary with TSMC, which would benefit from a growing customer and
application base, in addition to the company's existing IC design and IDM customers.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Global Unichip based on a PE methodology.
Downside risks include a slower-than-expected business recovery for mature nodes, lack of
new project wins, and wafer capacity constraints.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 121: 260922_ubs_memory smartphone

Source PDF: all report pdf/260922_ubs_memory smartphone.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-09-22
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 22 September 2026
Global Research
Global I/O
Smartphones
Memory to continue to weigh in on
Smartphones in 2027
Smartphone unit Sell-In -3% YoY in '27E; initial '28E forecasts at +2% YoY
UBS 3Q26 APAC Tech Tour takeaways ( link) continue to point towards (1) further
downside for Android ex-Samsung smartphone unit builds into 2027, and (2) relative
resilience for Apple and Samsung. Memory procurement could turn out to be
incrementally more challenging for smartphone OEMs in 2027 than in 2026. Memory

makers continue to prioritise hyperscale and AI customers. Hence memory supply could
be even more of a bottleneck next year. As such, we further de-risk our '27E
smartphone unit Sell-In forecasts to 1.11bn (-3% YoY) from 1.16bn (+2% YoY)
prior and roll out initial '28E forecasts at 1.13bn (+2% YoY). We leave our '26E
forecasts unchanged at 1.14bn (-10% YoY).
Memory costs continue to go higher
We now estimate memory to account for 35% of the bill of material (BOM) costs of

flagship/premium smartphones and 56% of those of low-end smartphones by 4Q26E
(from 13%/29% in 4Q25), based on our current base case scenario of the DRAM/NAND
upcycle extending until at least 2Q28E/4Q27E. This is broadly consistent with Xiaomi's
commentary that memory now accounts for 40% of average smartphone BOM costs
and 50-60% of low-end BOM costs. We believe the current memory cycle will remain a
key challenge for OEMs as (1) increasing costs continue to pressure margins, and (2) ASP

hikes aiming to offset such pressures are likely to pose incremental downside risk to end
demand. Over the long term, we believe cost control will be more manageable for Apple
and, to a lesser extent, Samsung, given their premium product portfolio, while more
detrimental for others.
Apple & Samsung outperformance likely to continue
Global smartphone unit Sell-Through (source: Counterpoint) is now down 8.4% YoY Jul
'26 YTD. Within this, we note Apple (+8.9% YoY) and Samsung (-4.8% YoY; Sell-In up

YoY implies some channel inventory build) continue to outperform their Chinese
Android peers (Vivo/Xiaomi/Oppo/Transsion: -16.4%/-17.9%/-18.4%/-22.4% YoY).
With premium models like to fare better in this downturn, we believe Apple and, to a
lesser extent, Samsung will continue to outperform the broader market and gain
incremental share. iPhone suppliers continue to expect '27E to be a strong year for
Apple, with the iPhone Duo (c.10m units in first 12M) as well as the new launch

schedule likely to drive YoY unit growth. Samsung continues to believe it will be able to
grow unit and gain market share, though profitability will remain a concern.
Selective on smartphone plays; prefer memory/MLCC suppliers
Within the smartphone supply chain, we continue to prefer MediaTek and Samsung
(both APAC Key Call Buy) as well as ASE, Hon Hai, JCET, Micron, Murata, SEMCO,
SK Hynix and TSMC (all Buy). We remain cautious on LG Display, Maxscend, Xiaomi (all

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
The handset industry, as part of consumer electronics, is cyclically sensitive to the economy
and so upside and downside risks relate to macro drivers. Other downside risks include the
rapid loss of market share due to poor product timing, design or distribution. The supply
chain companies into the handset industry also operate in a highly cyclical market both from
macro concerns and inventory cycles within the supply chain. These companies also operate

in a highly competitive environment with product innovation and R&D investments critical to
success. Valuation methods across the sector include multiple analysis.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.
EXCEPTIONS AND SPECIAL CASES:UK and European Investment Fund ratings and definitions are: Buy: Positive on factors
such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management,
performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the

## Document 123: 3008大立光 群益

Source PDF: all report pdf/3008大立光 群益.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-09-20
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 18 日

1 群益投顧 
大立光 (3008 TT)  公告 斥資新台幣 11.72 億元購入台中市南屯區約 2,259.25 坪 土 地，以
及約 3,695.25 坪 建 物 ， 供 未 來 擴 充 產 能 使 用， 這 是 大 立 光 今 年 第 7 度購買不動產 ， 7
筆合計金額約 80.78 億 元，目 的 皆 為 未 來 擴 充 產 能 需 求，預 期 09/2026 建 立 試 產 線 後 將
可推進多個客戶 Fiber Array 訂 單，並 在 1H27 量產 ；展望後勢 ，iPhone 鏡 頭 出 貨 進 入
傳 統 旺 季 ， 預 期 2H26 營 運 呈 現 逐 季 成 長 趨 勢 ， 法 說 會 上 明 示FAU 零 組 件 進 度 ， 預 期
1H27 可 望 量 產 ， 顯 示 公 司 對 FAU 零 組 件 業 務 表 示 高 度 信 心 ， 有 助 於 提 升 本 益 比 ， 維
持 Buy 投資建議 。

研 究 員 ： 劉 宜 和   
yiho.liu@capital.com.tw

大 立 光 今 年 累 計 7 次 出 手 購 地：  
大立光 (3008 TT) 公告 ，斥 資 新 台 幣 11.72 億 元 購 入 台 中 市 南 屯 區 約 2,259.25
坪 土 地 ， 以 及 約 3,695.25 坪 建 物 ， 供 未 來 擴 充 產 能 使 用 ， 這 是 大 立 光 今 年
第 7 度 購 買 不 動 產 ，7 筆 合 計 金 額 約 80.78 億 元 ， 目 的 皆 為 未 來 擴 充 產 能 需
求，預期 09/2026 建 立 試 產 線 後 將 可 推 進 多 個 客 戶 Fiber Array 訂 單 ， 並 在
1H27 量產 。  
展望 3Q26 ， 雖 然 iPhone18 標 準 版 手 機 鏡 頭 延 後 出 貨 ， 但 是 首 款 iPhone 摺
疊 機 開 賣 ， 以 及 可 變 光 圈 鏡 頭 較 傳 統7P 鏡 頭 漲 價 ， 皆 有 助 於 推 動 鏡 頭 旺 季
效 應 ， 預 估 3Q26 營收 178.89 億元， QoQ+30.92% ； 由 於 可 變 光 圈 鏡 頭 為 新
產 品 ， 又 因 增 加 多 個 檢 測 工 站 使 良 率 更 低 ， 毛 利 率 將 低 於2Q26 ， 預 估 稅 後
淨利 62.67 億 元 ， QoQ+34.19% ， EPS47.91 元 。  
展望 2026 年， Apple 摺 疊 機 發 售 可 望 減 緩 iPhone 銷 量 下 滑 的 幅 度 ， 力 拼 整
體 iPhone 銷 量 持 平 ；iPhone 半 導 體 零 組 件 多 為 自 製 ，iPhone 抵 抗 成 本 上 揚
能力優於 Android 手 機，故 能 依 照 原 定 計 畫 升 級 高 階 機 種 主 鏡 頭 為 可 變 光 圈
鏡頭，可變光圈鏡頭因為需要多段光圈皆能精準對焦，而增加檢測站數將
導 致 良 率 下 降，因 此 鏡 頭 價 格 較 傳 統 7P 鏡 頭 更 高，預 估 2026 年營收 653.47
億元， YoY+6.87% ， 稅 後 淨 利 238.33 億 元 ， YoY+12.02% ， EPS1 79.64 元。
未來 FAU 業 務 將 成 為 影 響 本 益 比 高 低 的 重 要 因 素。  
iPhone 鏡 頭 出 貨 進 入 傳 統 旺 季 ， 預 期2H26 營 運 呈 現 逐 季 成 長 趨 勢 ， 法 說 會
上明示 FAU 零 組 件 進 度 ， 預 期 1H27 可 望 量 產 ， 顯 示 公 司 對 FAU 零 組 件 業
務 表 示 高 度 信 心 ， 有 助 於 提 升 本 益 比 ， 維 持Buy 投 資 建 議 。

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 18 日

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係 「 首 次 」 給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時 ， 其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個
月目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為 四 個等級，定義如
上 。 爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」 或「區間操作」 之投資評
等，就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基
本 面 及 股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ，
研 判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。
我們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來
預測之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投
資 目 的 或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的 ，
既非對投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提
供詢價服務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值
及收益，可能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投
資 來 得 少 的 情 形 。
